programs a mismatched depth field, so none of them can see this.

*** BUT TWO PRIOR ROUNDS TOUCHED THIS EXACT CODE, AND THAT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT PREMISE IN
THIS BRIEF �?" PLEASE ATTACK IT. ***  Verbatim from `CHANGELOG.md`:

    ## #95 �?" central uninit-leak fix (Phase A) + #96-#100 (Phase B new-surface audit)
    **#95 `cpus/memory_rw.c`** (root-cause): zero the caller's buffer on every device READ before the
    len-clamp/dispatch, so a device handler that fills only part of `data` - or whose access is len-clamped -
    can never return uninitialised host memory to the guest. Closes the whole class (cf. per-device #91/93/94).

So: **`#95` claims to close "the whole class" of uninitialised-host-memory leaks, and `#100`,
in the same batch, edited THIS ARRAY.**  My claim is that two live members of that class
survive in the function `#100` edited.  If that is wrong I want to know now, not after a
round.  See Q1.

## 2. SETTLED PREMISES: none carried �?" first brief of this campaign.

## 3. THE CLAIM, stated as precisely as I can make it

`horrible_getputpixel()` declares

    uint8_t buf[8];		/* #100: bufdepth can be 8 = 1<<((mode>>8)&3) */

and **never initialises it.**  There is no `memset`, no initialiser, and (verified by grep)
the only `memset` calls in `dev_sgi_re.c` are on the device struct, on `rop_used`, and on
`zerobuf` �?" none of them on `buf`.

Two fields decide how much of `buf` is used.  They are decoded from the SAME guest-written
mode word and are **independent of each other**:

    uint32_t color_mode = mode & DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK;      /* bits 4-7  */
    int bufdepth = 1 << ((mode >> 8) & 3);               /* bits 8-9 -> 1, 2, 4 or 8 */

From `src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h`, inlined so you need not open it:

    #define DE_MODE_TYPE_CI		0x00000000
    #define DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB	0x00000010
    #define DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA	0x00000020
    #define DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR	0x00000030
    #define DE_MODE_TYPE_YCRCB	0x000000f0
    #define DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK	0x000000f0

**The colour arm decides how many bytes are FILLED; `bufdepth` decides how many are
TRANSFERRED.  Nothing makes them agree.**  `grep -rn '>> *8) *& *3' src/devices/` returns only
the decode itself, the `#100` comment, and the two XFER siblings �?" **no guard anywhere.**

### 3a. THE TWO PATHS.  Members enumerated, per AC4 �?" I am claiming exactly two.

**PATH A �?" PUT: uninitialised host stack written INTO guest-visible memory.**

| `color_mode` | bytes the arm assigns | assigned by nothing |
|---|---|---|
| `CI` (0x00)   | `buf[0]`      | `buf[1..7]` |
| `RGB` (0x10)  | `buf[0..3]`   | `buf[4..7]` |
| `RGBA` (0x20) | `buf[0..3]`   | `buf[4..7]` |
| `ABGR` (0x30) | `buf[0..3]`   | `buf[4..7]` |
| default (incl. `YCRCB` 0xf0) | `buf[0..3]` = `random()` | `buf[4..7]` |

then unconditionally:

    cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
        buf, bufdepth, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);

**With the depth field = 3 (`bufdepth` 8), NO colour arm writes `buf[4..7]` �?" not one of the
five, including the four correct ones �?" and all eight bytes are written.**  `CI` leaks at any
depth above 1.

**PATH B �?" GET: uninitialised host stack into `*color`, which the caller then STORES.**

    cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
        buf, bufdepth, MEM_READ, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
    switch (color_mode) {
    ...
    case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
        *color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8) + buf[3];

The read fills `bufdepth` bytes; the combine reads four.  **Depth field 0 (`bufdepth` 1) with
`RGBA` reads ONE byte from memory and builds a 32-bit colour out of four.**  `draw_primitive()`
then writes that colour to the destination in the same loop iteration �?" **the identical
caller-consumes-a-failed-read shape the `sgirandompixel` round just fixed one arm of**, reached
here with every colour mode documented and implemented and no undocumented value anywhere.

### 3b. WHY I BELIEVE `#95` DOES NOT COVER EITHER �?" please check this hardest

`#95` zeroes the caller's buffer **on every device READ**, for `len` bytes.

* **Path A is a `MEM_WRITE`.**  A read-side pre-zero cannot apply to it at all.
* **Path B is a `MEM_READ` of `bufdepth` bytes**, so `#95` zeroes `buf[0 .. bufdepth-1]`.  The
  bytes the combine then reads *beyond* `bufdepth` are outside `len` and untouched.

**If either of those is wrong the round is dead and I want it dead now.**

### 3c. Severity, stated honestly rather than at its most alarming

In a drawing loop the stale bytes are usually the PREVIOUS pixel's, so the everyday symptom is
**wrong colours / non-deterministic output**, not a dramatic host-secret disclosure.  On the
first call in a frame they are whatever the host stack held.  I am claiming a guest-reachable
uninitialised read whose value becomes guest-visible �?" **not** that it is a practical
exfiltration channel, and I would rather a seat talked me DOWN than up.  Both mode words are
ordinary device registers reached by ordinary stores, and all seven call sites pass one of them.

## 4. THE FULL ENCLOSING FUNCTION, verbatim (AD)

Frozen at `regress/artifacts/sgirebufuninit/enclosing_function.txt`, which is the version under
review.  Every exit path is present, including the early returns and the common tail.

```c
void horrible_getputpixel(int put, struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data* d,
	int x, int y, uint32_t* color, int mode)
{
	uint32_t color_mode = mode & DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK;
	int bufdepth = 1 << ((mode >> 8) & 3); 

	// dst_mode (see NetBSD's crmfbreg.h):
	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_A           0x00000000
	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_B           0x00000400
	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_C           0x00000800
	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_A           0x00001000
	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_B           0x00001400
	uint32_t tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7;
	int linear = tlb_mode > 3;

	if (!linear && (x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= 2048 || y >= 2048))
		return;

	int tilewidth_in_pixels = 512 / bufdepth;
	
	int tile_nr_x = x / tilewidth_in_pixels;
	int tile_nr_y = y >> 7;

	unsigned int tile_nr = tile_nr_y * 16 + tile_nr_x;

	y &= 127;
	int xofs = (x % tilewidth_in_pixels) * bufdepth;
	int ofs = 512 * y + xofs;

	uint32_t tileptr = 0;

	/*  #100: cases 0-2 index re_tlb_[abc][256] with a guest-derived
	    tile_nr = (y>>7)*16 + x/tilewidth; bound it (case 4 self-checks).  */
	if (tlb_mode <= 2 && tile_nr >= 256)
		return;

	switch (tlb_mode) {
	case 0:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_a[tile_nr] << 16;
		break;
	case 1:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_b[tile_nr] << 16;
		break;
	case 2:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_c[tile_nr] << 16;
		break;
	case 4:	tile_nr = x >> 12;
		if (tile_nr >= 32)
			return;
		tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[tile_nr] << 12);
		ofs = x & 4095;
		// TODO... probably not correct!
		// printf("tileptr = %08x x = %i y = %i tile_nr = %i ofs = %i\n", tileptr, x, y, tile_nr, ofs);
		break;
	default:/*  sgirandompixel: this arm used to assign `*color = random()' -- with
		    its exit(1) commented out -- so a failed GET handed the CALLER a
		    random value, and draw_primitive() then wrote it to the destination
		    in the same loop iteration, since `color' is shared by the XFER GET
		    and the PUT.  DE_MODE_LIN_B (0x1400) is one of the FIVE modes
		    crmfbreg.h documents and extracts to tlb_mode 5, so this was
		    reachable with a DOCUMENTED mode, not only with a junk one.
		    MEASURED, rung 3: the destination received 0x4b1763a1, 0x2ee4b4df
		    and 0x7433cdc1 on successive runs of
		    regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py.

		    NOW DECLINED: `*color' is left alone, so the caller's own
		    `uint32_t color = fg' stands and what lands is at least
		    DETERMINISTIC.  The announce is latched because this helper is
		    per-pixel.

		    WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, filed rather than silently omitted: the
		    caller STILL PERFORMS its dependent PUT, so a declined GET still
		    writes fg (row sgirandomskipput -- suppressing it needs a return
		    value and every GET site changed, which is more than one site);
		    LIN_B is still not implemented, and the panel refused to endorse
		    implementing it as LIN_A-with-the-other-array on symmetry alone
		    (row sgirelinb); and the COLOUR-mode switch below carries the same
		    random() shape at its own default (row sgirandomcolour).  */
		if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_BADMODE)) {
			d->warned |= SGIRE_W_BADMODE;
			/*  `dst_mode' is kept from the original text although the
			    parameter is also passed as src_mode on a GET: the probe
			    anchors on this literal, and rewording it here would be
			    scope creep AND would move an oracle anchor mid-round,
	o `memset`, no initialiser.  Is
   there an assignment I have missed �?" in the function, or a compiler-visible one?
2. **Is `#95`'s pre-zero really inapplicable?**  It zeroes the caller's buffer on device
   **READ** for `len` bytes.  Does it reach a `MEM_WRITE`?  Does it reach bytes at index
   >= `len`?  If it does either, PATH A or PATH B is dead.
3. **Is `tileptr + ofs` a DEVICE or is it RAM?**  If RAM, `#95` does not run at all and PATH B
   is worse than I said; if a device, the pre-zero covers `bufd_primitive()` or the XFER path �?" that constrains the pair before the call?
5. **Is depth field = 3 (`bufdepth` 8) actually reachable**, or does something upstream reject
   it?  `#100` grew the array *because* it believed 8 was reachable; that is evidence, not
   proof, and it is the premise PATH A rests on entirely.
6. **Is `DE_MODE_TYPE_YCRCB` (0xf0) genuinely unimplemented here?**  It is in the header's
   documented list and I see no `case` for it.

### Q2 �?" THE SMALLEST EDIT.  Name the row you expect to stay green.

Assume the fix is chosen and a detector ships with it.  **What is the smallest edit that
re-opens this defect and still passes a detector built the obvious way?**  I am especially
interested in edits that keep a `memset` but make it the wrong SIZE �?" `sizeof(buf)` vs
`bufdepth` vs a constant �?" because that is the shape a future refactor produces, and a
detector that only checks "the bytes are not stale" may not distinguish them.

### Q3 �?" OPTION-SET COMPLETENESS.  Grep the fork for the existing idiom.

The de-fatalisation option set does not obviously apply �?" nothing here is a refusal, and there
is no `exit()` to remove.  The dispositions I can name are:

1. **`memset(buf, 0, sizeof buf)` at declaration** �?" total, one line, and it costs a zeroing of
   8 bytes per pixel in a drawing loop.
2. **`memset(buf, 0, bufdepth)`** �?" covers PATH A exactly and leaves PATH B, since PATH B reads
   *beyond* `bufdepth`.
3. **Make the colour arms fill all `bufdepth` bytes** �?" i.e. treat the mismatch as the defect
   and pad per-arm.
4. **Decline the mismatched combination** (member 2/3: a mode whose colour type and depth
   disagree is a guest programming error) �?" with or without a latched announce.
5. **Clamp `bufdepth`** to what the colour mode needs.

**Is there a member I have not named?**  And which does the fork's own idiom favour �?" is there
an existing in-tree precedent for a device that reconciles two independent guest-chosen mode
fields?  Please grep rather than reason; the tree's precedents are part of the design space and
this project has twice found whole option-set members that way.

**Note the dispositions are NOT equivalent on observable behaviour**, which is the part I most
want checked: (1) and (2) make the guest see ZEROES, (3) makes it see a defined replication of
the colour, (4) makes it see the destination UNCHANGED, (5) makes it see a shorter transfer.
Those are five different guest-visible outcomes and I have no hardware evidence for any of
them.  **If the honest answer is "we do not know what the hardware does, so pick the one that
is not undefined behaviour and say so in the comment", say that** �?" it is the answer this
project has taken before and it is a legitimate one.

### Q4 �?" WITNESS SHAPE (the rung-3 attempt is mandatory before this can be documented-only)

`regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py` already programs this exact code path on an unmodified
committed `-E sgi -e ip32`, with both mandated controls green.  Its mode word is
`MODE_LIN_A = 0x1220` = LIN_A (bits 10-12) | depth field 2 (`bufdepth` 4) | RGBA (0x20) �?" the
MATCHED case, which leaks nothing.

**Is `0x1020` (the same line with depth field 0) sufficient to witness PATH B?**  And what is
the ORACLE?  The difficulty I see: an uninitialised read is not guaranteed to differ from the
correct value �?" the stale bytes may happen to be what a correct implementation would produce,
and in a loop they are often the previous pixel's.  **A witness whose oracle is "the value is
wrong" can pass by luck.**

I think the answer is to make the stale content KNOWN rather than to hope it differs: paint a
first pixel with a distinctive colour so `buf` holds known bytes, then read a second pixel at a
mismatched depth and assert the result contains bytes from the FIRST pixel that the second
pixel's memory does not hold.  **Is that sound, and is there a simpler oracle I am missing?**

### Q5 �?" SEVERITY.  Please talk me down if it is warranted.

I have graded this HIGH on the ledger.  Is that right?  The argument for lower: in a drawing
loop the stale bytes are usually the previous pixel's, so the practical symptom is wrong
colours rather than disclosure.  The argument for higher: it is guest-reachable through
documented registers with no undocumented value, and `#95` was a CRITICAL-class fix whose
claim to have closed the class is what this contradicts.

**If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE �?" say so.**

## 6. THE CLASS SCAN �?" a denominator for "is this systemic or specific?", run before firing this panel

I scanned `src/devices/*.c` for the signature and I am giving you the WHOLE result, not just the
hits that support me.  The scanner is `/tmp/scan_len.py`, reproduced here so you can judge it:

    call = re.compile(r'memory_rw\s*\((.*?)\)\s*;', re.S)
    for each *.c:
        bufs = names declared as `unsigned char|uint8_t NAME[<literal>];`
        for each memory_rw call whose 4th arg is one of those buffers:
            skip if the 5th arg (length) is sizeof(...) or a numeric literal
            report otherwise

**Result �?" SIX call sites in the whole device tree where the transfer length is not pinned to
the buffer:**

    file                         line buffer       length           dir
    dev_px.c                      151 dma_buf      dma_len          MEM_READ
    dev_px.c                      265 dma_buf      dma_len          MEM_READ
    dev_sgi_gbe.c                 263 buf          len              MEM_READ
    dev_sgi_re.c                  316 buf          bufdepth         MEM_WRITE   <- PATH A
    dev_sgi_re.c                  319 buf          bufdepth         MEM_READ    <- PATH B
    dev_sgi_re.c                 1462 zerobuf      fill_len         MEM_WRITE

**FOUR OF THE SIX ARE BOUNDED, AND I CHECKED EACH RATHER THAN ASSUMING:**

* **`dev_px.c` 151/265** �?" `dma_buf[32768]`, `dma_len = 4 * (5 + nspans*3)` where `nspans` is
  read out of guest memory.  That looks unbounded and **is not**: `nspans >>= 24` caps it at
  255, so `dma_len <= 3080`.  The span loop advances `ofs` by 12 per span, max 3080.  The
  `DEV_PX_TYPE_PXG` branch was separately bounded by `#207`.  **Safe, by a shift that is easy
  to miss.**
* **`dev_sgi_gbe.c` 263** �?" `buf[16384]`, `len` clamped by `if (len > 512) len = 512;` (OB-14).
  I also checked the two ways it could go wrong underneath that clamp: `len` cannot be
  NEGATIVE (`partial_pixels` derives from `((idata >> RHS_SHIFT) & 0x1f) * 32 ...`, so it is
  non-negative), and `bytes_per_pixel`, which is used as a DIVISOR at `(len / bytes_per_pixel)`,
  **cannot be zero** because `d->bitdepth = 8 << ((...) & 3)` yields 8/16/32/64.  **Safe.**
* **`dev_sgi_re.c` 1462** �?" `zerobuf` is `memset` before use.  **Safe.**

**So the answer is SPECIFIC, not systemic: the only unbounded-fill sites in the device tree are
the two this brief is about.**  That cuts both ways and I want it read both ways �?" it means the
round is small and bounded, and it also means **I have no corroborating instance**, so if my
reading of these two is wrong there is nothing else holding the finding up.

### THE SCANNER'S OWN LIMITS, stated so you can decide what it does NOT rule out

1. It splits arguments on commas, so a call with a nested comma inside an expression argument
   is mis-parsed and **silently skipped**.
2. It only matches buffers declared with a LITERAL size in the same file �?" not `malloc`ed
   ones, not ones reached through a struct, not VLAs.
3. **It tests the LENGTH, not the FILL.**  A site passing `sizeof(buf)` is excluded by
   construction �?" yet a `sizeof(buf)` transfer of a partially-filled buffer is the same defect.
   `dev_sgi_re.c:316` would have been MISSED had it been written `sizeof(buf)`.
4. It looks only at `memory_rw`; `memmove`/`memcpy` into guest-visible storage are not covered.

**Limit 3 is the one I would most like a seat to close**, because it is the direction that
would turn a specific finding into a class �?" and it is the direction my scanner is blind in.
If you can cheaply enumerate the partially-filled-buffer sites, that is worth more than
anything else in this brief.

### 6b. LIMIT 3 CLOSED �?" the partially-filled direction, scanned too

I said above that limit 3 was the one I most wanted closed, so I closed it rather than leaving
it for you.  Second scanner (`/tmp/scan_fill.py`): every `memory_rw` that **WRITES** a stack
byte-buffer into guest-visible memory, regardless of whether the length is `sizeof`, plus
whether the buffer is ever initialised wholesale.

    file                      line function                   buffer      size len            wholesale init?
    dev_scc.c                  305 dev_scc_dma_func           word           4 sizeof(word)   NO
    dev_sgi_mec.c              200 mec_try_rx                 data          32 sizeof(data)   yes
    dev_sgi_mec.c              370 mec_try_tx                 data          32 sizeof(data)   yes
    dev_sgi_re.c               316 horrible_getputpixel       buf            8 bufdepth       NO
    dev_sgi_re.c              1462 do_mte_transfer            zerobuf     4096 fill_len       yes

**It found one site the length scanner missed BY CONSTRUCTION** �?" `dev_scc.c:305` passes
`sizeof(word)`, which the first scanner excludes.  That is limit 3 demonstrated on a real site
rather than argued.

**I then opened all four non-`sgi_re` sites, and all four are SAFE �?" including the one my own
scanner flagged:**

* **`dev_scc.c:305` �?" FALSE POSITIVE, my scanner's fault.**  The bytes are assigned by a
  CHAINED assignment, `word[0] = word[1] = word[2] = word[3] = rx_nextchar(d, port);`, which my
  `memset`-or-initialiser test does not recognise.  All four bytes are set.  **Safe.**
* **`dev_sgi_mec.c:200` (`mec_try_rx`) �?"** `memset(data, 0, sizeof(data))` on a `data[8]`, i.e.
  a full clear, before the six assignments.  **Safe.**
* **`dev_sgi_mec.c:370` (`mec_try_tx`) �?"** this one deserved the look.  It is
  `memset(data, 0, 6)` on a `data[32]` and then writes `sizeof(data)` = **32** bytes, so bytes
  6..31 are NOT cleared.  They are not stale stack either: the function READ the same 32 bytes
  from the same address at the top and checked the result, so this is an intentional
  read-modify-write preserving the descriptor's tail (its comment says so �?" *"last 2 bytes are
  len"*).  **Safe.**
* **`dev_sgi_re.c:1462` �?"** `zerobuf` is `memset` before use.  **Safe.**

**A SCANNER DEFECT I FOUND WHILE CHECKING, which did not change any verdict but would have:**
the `size` column reports **32** for `mec_try_rx`, whose buffer is `data[8]`.  My declaration
map is keyed by NAME across the whole FILE, so two functions declaring `data` at different
sizes collapse to whichever was parsed last.  **A size-based conclusion from that column would
have been wrong**, and I am telling you because I nearly drew one.

> **BOTH SCANS AGREE: `dev_sgi_re.c` is the only member of this class in the device tree.**
> Six unpinned-length sites, five write-a-stack-buffer sites, and after opening every one of
> them the only defective site is the one this brief is about.

**Read that as evidence about SCOPE, not as corroboration.**  It means the round is small and
its blast radius is one function �?" and equally that **nothing else supports the finding**, so
if my reading of `#95` in A3b is wrong, there is no second instance holding it up.  That is why
A3b is the question I most want attacked.

## 7. REACHABILITY, DERIVED FROM THE INLINED FUNCTION �?" and the witness is one constant

I worked the mode arithmetic rather than asserting reachability.  **Please check the three
numbers**; they are the whole witness design.

`tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7`, so the LIN_A case (`tlb_mode == 4`) needs mode bits 10-12 =
`100b` = `0x1000`.  The depth field is bits 8-9; the colour type is bits 4-7.  Therefore:

| mode word | `tlb_mode` | depth field | `bufdepth` | `color_mode` | what it exercises |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x1220` | 4 (LIN_A) | 2 | **4** | RGBA | the MATCHED case �?" what the shipped probe uses; leaks nothing |
| `0x1320` | 4 (LIN_A) | 3 | **8** | RGBA | **PATH A** �?" arms fill `buf[0..3]`, eight bytes written |
| `0x1020` | 4 (LIN_A) | 0 | **1** | RGBA | **PATH B** �?" one byte read, four combined |

**LIN_A is the right vehicle and it is not a coincidence �?" it is the only case that cannot be
refused.**  Cases 0-2 take `tileptr` from `re_tlb_[abc][tile_nr]` and are then subject to

    if (!(tileptr & 0x80000000))
            return;

so the guest must first arrange the valid bit.  **Case 4 FORCES it:**
`tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[tile_nr] << 12)`.  So the LIN_A path reaches the
`buf` code unconditionally, which is why `regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py` already gets
there today with both mandated controls green.

**The consequence for cost: the witness is an existing, committed, gate-wired probe with ONE
CONSTANT CHANGED.**  That is why I am proposing this round now rather than filing it �?" it is
in the "easy resolvable" class, not because the defect is trivial but because the apparatus
already exists and is already trusted.

### The oracle problem is the real question, and I do not think I have solved it

An uninitialised read is not *guaranteed* to differ from a correct value.  In a loop the stale
bytes are usually the previous pixel's, so **an oracle of the form "the result is wrong" can
pass by luck** �?" which is the vacuity floor's own concern arriving before the fix exists.

My proposal, and I want it attacked rather than approved: **make the stale content KNOWN
instead of hoping it differs.**  Paint pixel 1 with a distinctive colour through the MATCHED
mode `0x1220` so `buf` demonstrably holds those bytes; then read pixel 2 at `0x1020`, where the
device supplies only `buf[0]`; then assert the resulting colour contains bytes from PIXEL 1
that pixel 2's own memory does not hold.  That is a positive identification of the leak rather
than an inequality.

**Two ways I can see it being wrong**, both of which a seat can settle faster than I can:

1. **`buf` may not survive between the two calls.**  It is a fresh stack frame each time.  The
   bytes usually persist because the frame is reused at the same depth, but *"usually"* is not
   an oracle �?" and a probe resting on it is a probe that passes for a reason it does not state.
   Is there a way to make the carry-over ARCHITECTURAL rather than incidental?
2. **`#95`'s pre-zero may already zero `buf[0..bufdepth-1]`** on each device read, which does
   not touch the bytes I care about �?" but if the destination is RAM rather than a device, no
   pre-zero runs at all, and the two cases give different stale content.  **Q1.3 above is
   therefore not a side question: it decides what the oracle can assert.**

If the honest answer is that PATH B cannot carry a deterministic oracle and only PATH A can,
say so �?" a round that witnesses one path and files the other with its reason is a better record
than one that claims both and proves one by luck.

### Q6 �?" SCOPE: is this ONE round or two?  (Added after the brief was drafted.)

Filed separately as `sgirandomcolour`, **in the same `switch` as PATH A**, is a second defect: that
switch's `default:` does `buf[0..3] = random()` and writes them.  Verified at HEAD �?" the switch
implements four `DE_MODE_TYPE_` cases, `crmfbreg.h` documents **five**, and
`grep -c YCRCB dev_sgi_re.c` is **0**, so the documented `YCRCB` (`0xf0`) lands there.

**They are different defects at the same lines:** `sgirebufuninit` is bytes *nobody assigns*;
`sgirandomcolour` is bytes assigned *from `random()`*. **Fixing either leaves the other standing** �?"
a `memset` does not stop the `default:` arm overwriting with `random()`, and removing the `random()`
does not fill `buf[4..7]`.

The reopening rule admits a second site only if it is witnessed, same device, same semantic class
so one fix design covers both, and **covered by the same detector with no new oracle**.
`sgirandomcolour` has **no witness**, so on the letter it cannot join.

**My question is whether the letter is right here.** Both make the guest receive non-deterministic
data from one function, and a single oracle �?" *the destination is DETERMINISTIC across runs* �?"
plausibly covers both. If so, one round with one detector is cheaper and leaves no half-fixed
switch. If not, say so and I will take them separately.

**Please do not let the convenience of one round drive the answer**; a shared oracle that is weaker
than two purpose-built ones is the vacuity floor's problem, not a saving.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

import re, glob, os
#  The sgirebufuninit signature: a stack buffer passed to memory_rw with a length that is
#  NOT sizeof(buf) and not a literal -- i.e. a length the GUEST may influence, where the
#  fill and the transfer can disagree.
call = re.compile(r'memory_rw\s*\((.*?)\)\s*;', re.S)
hits = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob('*.c')):
    src = open(f, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read()
    # stack byte buffers declared in this file
    bufs = set(re.findall(r'\b(?:unsigned char|uint8_t)\s+(\w+)\s*\[\s*\d+\s*\]\s*;', src))
    if not bufs:
        continue
    for m in call.finditer(src):
        args = [a.strip() for a in m.group(1).split(',')]
        if len(args) < 6:
            continue
        databuf, ln = args[3], args[4]
        nm = re.sub(r'^&', '', databuf).split('[')[0]
        if nm not in bufs:
            continue
        if ln.startswith('sizeof') or re.fullmatch(r'(0x)?[0-9]+', ln):
            continue          # transfer length pinned to the buffer -> not this class
        line = src[:m.start()].count('\n') + 1
        hits.append((f, line, nm, ln, args[5]))
print("%-26s %6s %-12s %-16s %s" % ("file", "line", "buffer", "length", "dir"))
for h in hits:
    print("%-26s %6d %-12s %-16s %s" % h)
print("\n%d call sites where the transfer length is NOT pinned to the buffer" % len(hits))

import re, glob
#  LIMIT 3 of the length scanner: a site passing sizeof(buf) is excluded by construction, yet a
#  sizeof(buf) transfer of a PARTIALLY FILLED buffer is the same defect.  This closes that
#  direction: every memory_rw that WRITES a stack byte-buffer into guest-visible memory, with
#  whether the enclosing function ever initialises that buffer wholesale.
call = re.compile(r'memory_rw\s*\((.*?)\)\s*;', re.S)
fn   = re.compile(r'^[A-Za-z_][\w \t\*]*\b(\w+)\s*\([^;]*\)\s*\{', re.M)
rows = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob('*.c')):
    src = open(f, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read()
    decls = dict(re.findall(r'\b(?:unsigned char|uint8_t)\s+(\w+)\s*\[\s*(\d+)\s*\]\s*;', src))
    if not decls: continue
    fns = [(m.start(), m.group(1)) for m in fn.finditer(src)]
    for m in call.finditer(src):
        args = [a.strip() for a in m.group(1).split(',')]
        if len(args) < 6: continue
        nm = re.sub(r'^&', '', args[3]).split('[')[0]
        if nm not in decls: continue
        if 'MEM_WRITE' not in args[5]: continue      # writes INTO guest-visible memory
        owner = 'file-scope'
        for pos, name in fns:
            if pos < m.start(): owner = name
        # does anything initialise the whole buffer?
        init = bool(re.search(r'memset\s*\(\s*&?' + nm + r'\b', src)) or \
               bool(re.search(r'\b(?:unsigned char|uint8_t)\s+' + nm + r'\s*\[\s*\d+\s*\]\s*=', src))
        line = src[:m.start()].count('\n') + 1
        rows.append((f, line, owner, nm, decls[nm], args[4], "yes" if init else "NO"))
print("%-24s %5s %-26s %-10s %5s %-14s %s" % ("file","line","function","buffer","size","len","wholesale init?"))
for r in rows: print("%-24s %5d %-26s %-10s %5s %-14s %s" % r)
print("\n%d MEM_WRITE sites from a stack byte-buffer; %d with NO wholesale initialisation"
      % (len(rows), sum(1 for r in rows if r[6] == "NO")))

3:#  sgirandompixel -- RUNG-3 WITNESS for dev_sgi_re.c horrible_getputpixel().
5:#  THE DEFECT.  horrible_getputpixel() extracts `tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7' and switches on it.
7:#  and then executes `*color = random(); return;'.  So a failed GET does not decline -- it hands the
8:#  caller a random 32-bit value.  In draw_primitive() the failed source GET and the destination PUT
11:#      if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN)
12:#              horrible_getputpixel(0, ..., src_x, src_y, &color, src_mode);   // may set random()
15:#              horrible_getputpixel(1, ..., x, y, &color, dst_mode);           // writes it
18:#    DE_MODE_TLB_A 0x0000 -> 0 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_A 0x1000 -> 4 handled
19:#    DE_MODE_TLB_B 0x0400 -> 1 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_B 0x1400 -> 5 *** default -> random() ***
20:#    DE_MODE_TLB_C 0x0800 -> 2 handled
24:#              destination receives anything other than a value distinct from BOTH the source and
25:#              the pre-painted destination.  Restoring `exit(1)' there kills the machine and reds
27:#              ONLY if the pre-paint is removed -- which is why the destination is PRE-PAINTED with a
28:#              known value and the row rejects it: "left alone" and "wrote random" are then distinct.
35:#  set the source mode to LIN_B, draw, read the destination -- FINDS NOTHING.  At reset the
36:#  destination mode is TLB_A and re_tlb_a[] has bit 31 clear, so the PUT returns WITHOUT WRITING and
37:#  the destination is unchanged: a false negative that looks like a clean run.  Using LIN_A as the
42:#  THE ORACLE IS CERTAIN, NOT PROBABILISTIC.  glibc random() is 31-bit, so the value written has bit
43:#  31 CLEAR and can never equal a source of 0xAABBCCDD.  Inequality is guaranteed, not 2^-32.
45:#  usage: sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
63:DE_MODE_SRC   = 0xb5002000
64:DE_MODE_DST   = 0xb5002008
73:MODE_LIN_A = 0x1220               # LIN_A 0x1000 | BUFDEPTH_32 0x200 | TYPE_RGBA 0x20
74:MODE_LIN_B = 0x1620               # LIN_B 0x1400 | BUFDEPTH_32 0x200 | TYPE_RGBA 0x20
82:SRC_VAL    = 0xaabbccdd           # bit 31 SET: random() (31-bit) can never equal it
86:                                  # observation and the oracle is vacuous by construction.

27: *
28: *  Generic memory_rw(), with special hacks for specific CPU families.
29: *
30: *  Example for inclusion from memory_mips.c:
31: *
32: *	MEMORY_RW should be mips_memory_rw
33: *	MEM_MIPS should be defined
34: *
35: *
36: *  TODO: Cleanup the "ok" variable usage!
37: */
38:
39:
40:/*
41: *  memory_rw():
42: *
43: *  Read or write data from/to memory.
44: *
45: *	cpu		the cpu doing the read/write
46: *	mem		the memory object to use
47: *	vaddr		the virtual address
48: *	data		a pointer to the data to be written to memory, or
49: *			a placeholder for data when reading from memory
50: *	len		the length of the 'data' buffer
51: *	writeflag	set to MEM_READ or MEM_WRITE
52: *	misc_flags	CACHE_{NONE,DATA,INSTRUCTION} | other flags
53: *
54: *  If the address indicates access to a memory mapped device, that device'
55: *  read/write access function is called.
56: *
57: *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
58: *
59: *  Returns one of the following:
60: *	MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED
61: *	MEMORY_ACCESS_OK
62: *
63: *  (MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED is 0.)
64: */
65:
66:/*
67: *  #430: THE PAGE SIZE, IN ONE PLACE.
68: *
69: *  It was a local inside the function.  The wrapper below needs the same number, and two
70: *  copies of a page size that can silently diverge is the shape this tree has been bitten
71: *  by before -- so it is hoisted to a macro and the local now reads it.  Note the Alpha
72: *  value: it is 8 KB, not 4 KB, so anything that splits at "the page boundary" must use
73: *  THIS and never a literal 4096.  A review seat caught exactly that hazard in the design
74: *  brief, which had said "4 KB page" throughout.
75: */
76:#ifdef MEM_ALPHA
77:#define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0x1fff
78:#else
79:#define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0xfff
80:#endif
81:
82:/*
83: *  #430: THE SPLIT GRANULE IS 1 KB, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME NUMBER AS THE MASK ABOVE.
84: *
85: *  MRW_OFFSET_MASK is the HOST-page granule: what memory_paddr_to_hostaddr() is asked for
86: *  and what update_translation_table() caches.  The granule the SPLIT needs is a different
87: *  thing -- the smallest unit at which a translator in this tree can send two adjacent
88: *  virtual addresses to unrelated physical ones -- and it is SMALLER:
89: *
90: *      memory_arm.c:198-207   descriptor type 3 on a non-XScale core is a 1 KB page,
91: *                             *return_paddr = (d2 & 0xfffffc00) | (vaddr & 0x3ff)
92: *      memory_sh.c:116        SH4_PTEL_SZ_1K gives mask 0xfffffc00
93: *      memory_mips_v2p.c      the VR41xx path likewise has 1 KB logic
94: *
95: *  A first draft of this split used the 4 KB mask and a review seat produced the
96: *  counterexample: with a 1 KB mapping, a four-byte access at 0x13fe crosses a translation
97: *  boundary but NOT a 4 KB one, so the loop never fired and the defect survived exactly
98: *  where MEMORY_NOT_FULL_PAGE says it is most likely.  Splitting finer is always SAFE --
99: *  each chunk is then inside one mapping for every translator here, and the worker's own
100: *  host-page arithmetic is unchanged -- so the only cost is more worker calls on the slow
101: *  path for a multi-page translated access, which is rare (large transfers are PHYSICAL
102: *  and exempt).  Correctness at a known granule beats a faster one that is wrong on ARM.
103: */
104:#define	MRW_SPLIT_MASK		0x3ff
105:
106:/*  The one-page worker; its name is derived from MEMORY_RW because cpu_alpha.c includes
107:    this file TWICE in one translation unit (alpha_userland_memory_rw, then alpha_memory_rw
108:    via tmp_alpha_tail.c), so a plainly named static would be a redefinition.  */
109:#ifndef MRW_CAT2
110:#define	MRW_CAT2(a,b)		a##b
111:#define	MRW_CAT(a,b)		MRW_CAT2(a,b)
112:#endif
113:#define	MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE	MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _one_page)
114:
115:/*
116: *  The one relationship between the two that MUST hold, asserted at COMPILE TIME because it
117: *  cannot be asserted at run time by the offline differential.
118: *
119: *  MEM_ALPHA is never compiled by that differential -- its fixture is 4 KB-specific -- so a
120: *  review seat MEASURED that widening MRW_OFFSET_MASK, or hardcoding the worker's local back
121: *  to 0xfff (exactly the drift this macro exists to prevent), leaves every row green.  A
122: *  static assertion costs nothing and covers the Alpha build the test cannot reach.  The
123: *  name is derived because this file is included twice per translation unit.
124: */

58:/*
59: *  #497: one latch bit per WIDTH-guard site in DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re).
60: *
61: *  SIX FLAGS, NOT ONE.  dev_wdc.c's own comment adjudicates this: sharing a
62: *  latch is licensed only across sites that are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE at
63: *  runtime.  These six are six different address windows, and any driver
64: *  bringing up the rendering engine walks all of them in ONE session, so a
65: *  shared latch would report the first odd-width access and silence the
66: *  other five.
67: */
68:enum {
69:	SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN  = 1 << 0,
70:	SGIRE_W_TLBB_LEN  = 1 << 1,
71:	SGIRE_W_TLBC_LEN  = 1 << 2,
72:	SGIRE_W_TEX_LEN   = 1 << 3,
73:	SGIRE_W_LINA_LEN  = 1 << 4,
74:	SGIRE_W_LINB_LEN  = 1 << 5,
75:
76:	/*  #498: sgi_de's width decline.  ONE bit, not six: the six above are one
77:	    per SITE because six windows with six distinct message texts are walked
78:	    in a single bring-up session, and one latch would report the first and
79:	    silence five.  sgi_de has ONE guard and ONE message text, so the house
80:	    per-message-site rule gives one bit.  Measured cost: `sb' then `sh' at
81:	    one offset announces len=1 and never len=2 -- accepted, and small,
82:	    because the message already prints the width.
83:
84:	    It must be its OWN bit.  Reusing SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN scored 23/25: the
85:	    four handlers share one malloc'd struct, so any odd-width access to
86:	    sgi_re's TLB_A window would silence sgi_de's decline entirely.  */
87:	SGIRE_W_DE_LEN    = 1 << 6,
88:
89:	/*  #499: the unmatched-address catch-all.  TWO bits, one per DIRECTION:
90:	    a driver probing an unmapped window commonly reads and writes it in
91:	    one pass, and one bit would announce the first and silence the other.
92:
93:	    PLACED AFTER SGIRE_W_DE_LEN ON PURPOSE.  An earlier draft inserted
94:	    these two after SGIRE_W_LINB_LEN (1 << 5), which left the enum ending
95:	    at 1 << 6 -- so a maintainer scanning to the LAST enumerator for the
96:	    next free bit would take 1 << 7, WHICH THESE TWO LINES BELOW ALREADY
97:	    USE.  A duplicate enumerator is legal C and silent, and all four
98:	    handlers share one malloc'd struct, so the collision crosses devices.
99:
100:	    (Wording sharpened after a pass-2 seat read "which is already used"
101:	    as a standalone claim about 1 << 7 and reported the comment as
102:	    self-contradictory against the code three lines down.  The claim was
103:	    right and the sentence did not say BY WHAT.)  */
104:	SGIRE_W_UNMAP_WR  = 1 << 7,
105:	SGIRE_W_UNMAP_RD  = 1 << 8,
106:
107:	/*  #500: draw_primitive()'s unimplemented drawing op.  ITS OWN BIT,
108:	    for the reason #498 measured on SGIRE_W_DE_LEN: a latch shared with
109:	    another site is silenced by that site's ordinary traffic, so the
110:	    announce this fix exists to make would never fire.
111:
112:	    (This comment used to end "The next free bit is 1 << 10; the enum
113:	    ends HERE on purpose so that a maintainer scanning to the last
114:	    enumerator finds it."  BOTH HALVES WENT FALSE when sgirandompixel
115:	    added SGIRE_W_BADMODE = 1 << 10 BELOW this line.  Demoted rather
116:	    than deleted, because the REASONING is still why the convention
117:	    exists -- and leaving it live would have pointed the next reader at
118:	    a taken bit, which is the duplicate-enumerator collision #499
119:	    already paid for and warns about four lines up.  The live
120:	    next-free claim is on the LAST enumerator, where it belongs.)  */
121:	SGIRE_W_PRIM_OP   = 1 << 9,
122:
123:	/*  sgirandompixel: horrible_getputpixel()'s unimplemented-mode arm.  ITS
124:	    OWN BIT, for the reason #498 measured and #500 repeated -- the four
125:	    handlers share one malloc'd struct, so a latch shared with another
126:	    site is silenced by that site's ordinary traffic and the announce
127:	    never fires.  This one matters more than most: the helper is
128:	    PER-PIXEL, called inside line, rectangle and tiled-MTE loops, so an
129:	    UNLATCHED announce is a per-pixel flood rather than a diagnostic.
130:	    The next free bit is 1 << 11; the enum ends HERE on purpose, as
131:	    above.  */
132:	SGIRE_W_BADMODE   = 1 << 10,
133:};
134:
135:struct sgi_re_data {
136:	// Rendering engine registers:
137:	uint16_t	re_tlb_a[256];
138:	uint16_t	re_tlb_b[256];
139:	uint16_t	re_tlb_c[256];
140:	uint16_t	re_tex[112];
141:	// todo: clip_ids registers.
142:	uint32_t	re_linear_a[32];
143:	uint32_t	re_linear_b[32];
144:
145:	// Drawing engine registers:
146:	uint32_t	de_reg[DEV_SGI_DE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
147:
148:	// Memory transfer engine registers:
149:	uint32_t	mte_reg[DEV_SGI_MTE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
150:
151:	/*
152:	 *  #497: width-guard decline latches; see the enum above.
153:	 *
154:	 *  PLACED LAST, DELIBERATELY, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.
155:	 *  re_linear_a/b are written at [tlbi] AND [tlbi+1] with tlbi able to
156:	 *  reach 31, so index 32 of either array lands on whichever field
157:	 *  FOLLOWS it.  An earlier draft of this change put `warned' between
158:	 *  re_linear_b and de_reg; measured with offsetof, that made
159:	 *  re_linear_b[32] alias `warned' rather than de_reg[0] -- which both
160:	 *  falsified the adjacency the two LINEAR site comments cite, and made
161:	 *  an over-run SET WARNING LATCH BITS and silence this device's own
162:	 *  diagnostics.  Keeping the latch after mte_reg restores the
163:	 *  documented adjacency: re_linear_a[32] is re_linear_b[0] and
164:	 *  re_linear_b[32] is de_reg[0].
165:	 */
166:	uint32_t	warned;
167:};
168:
169:
170:/*
171: *  horrible_getputpixel():
172: *
173: *  This routine gets/puts a pixel in one of the tiles, from the perspective of
174: *  the rendering/drawing engine. Given x and y, it figures out which tile
175: *  number it is, and then finally does a slow read/write to get/put the pixel
176: *  at the correct sub-coordinates within the tile.
177: *
178: *  Tiles are always 512 _bytes_ wide, and 128 pixels high. For 32-bit color
179: *  modes, for example, that means 128 x 128 pixels.
180: *
181: *  For "linear" modes, y is ignored and x is an offset to select which linear
182: *  TLB entry to use.
183: */
184:void horrible_getputpixel(int put, struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data* d,
350:	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
351:
352:	idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
353:
354:	relative_addr += 0x1000;
355:
356:	/*
357:	 *  #497: THE SIX WIDTH GUARDS BELOW USED TO END THE HOST PROCESS.
358:	 *
359:	 *  A guest choosing an access width is not an emulator error, so each
360:	 *  `len != 8' arm now DECLINES the access and announces itself once.
361:	 *  Three properties of the shape are load-bearing:
362:	 *
363:	 *  1. It is an `else if' SPLICE, not an early return.  A `return' here
364:	 *     would skip this function's common tail, and `return 0' would be
365:	 *     coerced to -1 by cpus/memory_rw.c and raise EXCEPTION_DBE in the
366:	 *     guest -- turning a declined access into a guest bus error.
367:	 *
368:	 *  2. Control therefore reaches the tail, which answers a declined READ
369:	 *     with odata (0).
370:	 *
371:	 *     *** THIS PARAGRAPH ONCE CALLED THAT TAIL DEAD CODE -- "every read arm
372:	 *     below still exit(1)s, so odata is never assigned today".  #499 MADE IT
373:	 *     LIVE: the unmatched-address catch-all no longer exits, so a declined
374:	 *     read now runs the tail and lands a defined 0, and the shipped detector's
375:	 *     `rd sent' row exists to pin exactly that.  The six `TODO: read from'
376:	 *     arms below DO still exit(1) -- that half is unchanged and is sgirekills. ***
377:	 *
378:	 *  3. The guards are NOT redundant.  For TLB_A/B/C and TEX the store is a
379:	 *     `for (hwi = 0; hwi < len; ...)' loop, so a narrower access simply
380:	 *     runs fewer iterations and stays in bounds -- there the guard is
381:	 *     modelling fidelity only.  For LINEAR_A and LINEAR_B it is not; see
382:	 *     the note at those two sites.
383:	 *
384:	 *  Only the WIDTH kills change HERE, IN #497.  The six `TODO: read from ...'
385:	 *  kills are still untouched.
386:	 *
387:	 *  *** THE CATCH-ALL IS NO LONGER UNTOUCHED: #499 DE-FATALISED IT and that row
388:	 *  is CLOSED.  The sentence is #497's and was true when written; it is left
389:	 *  standing with its correction rather than rewritten, because a reader
390:	 *  arriving from #497's records needs to see which half moved. ***  The READ
391:	 *  arms are
392:	 *  `sgirekills`, the CATCH-ALL is `sgireunmapped` (two different rows: one is a
393:	 *  direction, the other an unmatched address with no width predicate at all), as is
394:	 *  DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_de)'s own width kill, whose accept set is {4,8}
395:	 *  rather than {8} (sgidewidth).
396:	 */
397:	if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TLB_A && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TLB_B) {
398:		if (len != 8) {
399:			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN)) {
400:				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBA_LEN;
401:				fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for "
402:				    "CRIME_RE_TLB_A -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
403:				    (int) len);
404:			}
405:		} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
406:			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
407:			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
408:				d->re_tlb_a[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
409:				debug("d->re_tlb_a[%i] = 0x%04x\n", tlbi, d->re_tlb_a[tlbi]);
410:				tlbi++;
411:			}
412:		} else {
413:			fatal("TODO: read from CRIME_RE_TLB_A\n");
414:			exit(1);
415:		}
416:	} else if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TLB_B && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TLB_C) {
417:		if (len != 8) {
418:			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBB_LEN)) {
419:				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBB_LEN;
420:				fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for "
421:				    "CRIME_RE_TLB_B -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
422:				    (int) len);
423:			}
424:		} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
425:			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
426:			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
427:				d->re_tlb_b[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
428:				debug("d->re_tlb_b[%i] = 0x%04x\n", tlbi, d->re_tlb_b[tlbi]);
429:				tlbi++;
430:			}
431:		} else {
432:			fatal("TODO: read from CRIME_RE_TLB_B\n");
433:			exit(1);
434:		}
435:	} else if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TLB_C && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TLB_C + 0x200) {
436:		if (len != 8) {
437:			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TLBC_LEN)) {
438:				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TLBC_LEN;
439:				fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for "
440:				    "CRIME_RE_TLB_C -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
441:				    (int) len);
442:			}
443:		} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
444:			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0x1ff) >> 1) & 0xff;
445:			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
446:				d->re_tlb_c[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
447:				debug("d->re_tlb_c[%i] = 0x%04x\n", tlbi, d->re_tlb_c[tlbi]);
448:				tlbi++;
449:			}
450:		} else {
451:			fatal("TODO: read from CRIME_RE_TLB_C\n");
452:			exit(1);
453:		}
454:	} else if (relative_addr >= CRIME_RE_TEX && relative_addr < CRIME_RE_TEX + 0xe0) {
455:		if (len != 8) {
456:			if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_TEX_LEN)) {
457:				d->warned |= SGIRE_W_TEX_LEN;
458:				fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented len=%i for "
459:				    "CRIME_RE_TEX -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
460:				    (int) len);
461:			}
462:		} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
463:			int tlbi = ((relative_addr & 0xff) >> 3) & 0xff;
464:			for (size_t hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += sizeof(uint16_t)) {
465:				d->re_tex[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
466:				debug("d->re_tex[%i] = 0x%04x\n", tlbi, d->re_tex[tlbi]);
467:				tlbi++;
468:			}
469:		} else {
470:			fatal("TODO: read from CRIME_RE_TEX\n");
665:}
666:
667:
668:
669:/****************************************************************************/
670:
671:/*
672: *  SGI "de", NetBSD sources describes it as a "drawing engine".
673: */
674:
675:void draw_primitive(struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data *d)
676:{
677:	uint32_t op = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_PRIMITIVE - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
678:	uint32_t drawmode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_DRAWMODE - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
679:	uint32_t dst_mode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_MODE_DST - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
680:	uint32_t src_mode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_MODE_SRC - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
681:	uint32_t fg = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_FG - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
682:	uint32_t bg = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_BG - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
683:	uint32_t rop = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_ROP - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
684:
685:	uint32_t stipple_mode = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_MODE - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
686:	uint32_t pattern = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_STIPPLE_PAT - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
687:	int nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_left = (stipple_mode >> DE_STIP_STRTIDX_SHIFT) & 31;
688:	int nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right = 31 - ((stipple_mode >> DE_STIP_MAXIDX_SHIFT) & 31);
689:	pattern >>= nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right;
690:	pattern <<= nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right;
691:	pattern <<= nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_left;
692:
693:	int nr_of_bits_in_the_middle = 32 - nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_left - nr_of_bits_to_strip_to_the_right;
694:
695:	if (stipple_mode & 0xe0e0ffff)
696:		fatal("[ sgi_de: UNIMPLEMENTED stipple_mode bits: 0x%08x ]\n", stipple_mode);
697:
698:	uint32_t x1 = (d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_0 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)] >> 16) & 0x7ff;
699:	uint32_t y1 = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_0 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)]& 0x7ff;
700:	uint32_t x2 = (d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_1 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)] >> 16) & 0x7ff;
701:	uint32_t y2 = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_X_VERTEX_1 - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)]& 0x7ff;
702:	size_t x, y;
703:
704:	debug("[ sgi_de: STARTING DRAWING COMMAND: op = 0x%08x,"
705:	    " drawmode=0x%x src_mode=0x%x dst_mode=0x%x x1=%i y1=%i"
706:	    " x2=%i y2=%i fg=0x%x bg=0x%x pattern=0x%08x ]\n",
707:	    op, drawmode, src_mode, dst_mode, x1, y1, x2, y2, fg, bg, pattern);
708:
709:	// bufdepth = 1, 2, or 4.
710:	// int dst_bufdepth = 1 << ((dst_mode >> 8) & 3);
711:	int src_bufdepth = 1 << ((src_mode >> 8) & 3);
712:
713:	int src_is_linear = 0;
714:	int src_x = -1, src_y = -1;
715:	int32_t step_x = 0;
716:	if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN) {
717:		uint32_t addr_src = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_SRC - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
718:		uint32_t strd_src = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_SRC - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
719:		step_x = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_X - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
720:		int32_t step_y = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STEP_Y - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
721:		uint32_t addr_dst = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_ADDR_DST - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
722:		uint32_t strd_dst = d->de_reg[(CRIME_DE_XFER_STRD_DST - 0x2000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
723:
724:		src_is_linear = ((src_mode & 0x00001c00) >> 10) > 3;
725:
726:		if (src_is_linear) {
727:			src_x = addr_src;
728:			src_y = 0;
729:		} else {
730:			src_x = (addr_src >> 16) & 0x7ff;
731:			src_y = addr_src & 0x7ff;
732:		}
733:
734:		if (step_x != src_bufdepth || (step_y != 0 && step_y != 1)) {
735:			fatal("[ sgi_de: unimplemented XFER addr_src=0x%x src_bufdepth=%i "
736:				"strd_src=0x%x step_x=0x%x step_y=0x%x "
737:				"addr_dst=0x%x strd_dst=0x%x ]\n",
738:				addr_src, src_bufdepth, strd_src, step_x, step_y, addr_dst, strd_dst);
739:
740:			// exit(1);
741:		}
742:	}
743:
744:	if (!(drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_PLANEMASK)) {
745:		printf("!DE_DRAWMODE_PLANEMASK: TODO\n");
746:	}
747:	
748:	if ((drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_BYTEMASK) != DE_DRAWMODE_BYTEMASK) {
749:		printf("not all DE_DRAWMODE_BYTEMASK set: TODO\n");
750:	}
751:	
752:	// primitive rendering direction (not for Lines? and presumably
753:	// not for Points either :-)
754:	int dx = op & DE_PRIM_RL ? -1 :  1;
755:	int dy = op & DE_PRIM_TB ?  1 : -1;
756:
757:	uint16_t saved_src_x = src_x;
758:
759:	/*
760:	 *  Drawing is limited to 2048 x 2048 pixel space.
761:	 *
762:	 *  TODO: MAYBE it is really -2048 to 2027, i.e. 12 bits of
763:	 *  pixel space. Perhaps it is possible to figure out
764:	 *  experimentally some day, e.g. by drawing lines from
765:	 *  -10,10 to 500,20 and see whether the real hardware
766:	 *  interprets -10 as -10 or 0x800 - 10.
767:	 */
768:	uint16_t endx = (x2 + dx) & 0x7ff;
769:	uint16_t endy = (y2 + dy) & 0x7ff;
770:
771:	int lx = abs((int)(x2 - x1)), ly = abs((int)(y2 - y1));
772:	int linelen = lx > ly ? lx : ly;
773:
774:	switch (op & 0xff000000) {
775:	case DE_PRIM_LINE:
776:		if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN)
777:			fatal("[ sgi_de: XFER_EN for LINE op? ]\n");
778:
779:		// The PROM uses width 32, but NetBSD documents it as "half pixels".
780:		// if ((op & DE_PRIM_LINE_WIDTH_MASK) != 2)
781:		//	fatal("[ sgi_de: LINE_WIDTH_MASK = %i ]\n", op & DE_PRIM_LINE_WIDTH_MASK);
782:
783:		if (linelen == 0)
784:			linelen ++;
785:
786:		for (int i = 0; i < ((op & DE_PRIM_LINE_SKIP_END)? linelen : linelen+1); ++i) {
787:			x = (x2 * i + x1 * (linelen-i)) / linelen;
788:			y = (y2 * i + y1 * (linelen-i)) / linelen;
789:
790:			uint32_t color = fg;
791:			uint32_t oldcolor = fg;
792:
793:			if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_ROP && rop != OPENGL_LOGIC_OP_COPY)
794:				horrible_getputpixel(0, cpu, d, x, y, &oldcolor, dst_mode);
795:
796:			int draw = 1;
797:			if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_LINE_STIP) {
798:				if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_OPAQUE_STIP)
799:					color = (pattern & 0x80000000UL) ? fg : bg;
800:				else
801:					draw = (pattern & 0x80000000UL)? 1 : 0;
802:			}
803:
804:			// Raster-OP.
805:			// TODO: Other ops.
806:			// TODO: Should this be before or after other things?
807:			if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_ROP) {
808:				switch (rop) {
809:				case OPENGL_LOGIC_OP_COPY:
810:					// color = color;
811:					break;
812:				case OPENGL_LOGIC_OP_XOR:
813:					color = oldcolor ^ color;
814:					break;
815:				case OPENGL_LOGIC_OP_COPY_INVERTED:
816:					color = 0xffffffff - oldcolor;
817:					break;
818:				default:{
819:						static char rop_used[256];
820:						static int first = 1;
821:
822:						if (first) {
823:							memset(rop_used, 0, sizeof(rop_used));
824:							first = 0;
825:						}
826:
827:						if (!rop_used[rop & 255]) {
828:							rop_used[rop & 255] = 1;
829:							fatal("[ sgi_de: LINE: rop[0x%02x] used! ]\n", rop & 255);
830:						}
831:
832:						if (rop >> 8) {
833:							fatal("[ sgi_de: LINE: rop > 255: 0x%08x ]\n", rop);
834:						}
835:					}
836:				}
837:			}
838:
839:			if (draw)
840:				horrible_getputpixel(1, cpu, d, x, y, &color, dst_mode);
841:
842:			// Rotate the stipple pattern:
843:			pattern = (pattern << 1) | (pattern >> (nr_of_bits_in_the_middle-1));
844:		}
845:		break;
846:
847:	case DE_PRIM_RECTANGLE:
848:		for (y = y1; y != endy; y = (y + dy) & 0x7ff) {
849:			src_x = saved_src_x;
850:			for (x = x1; x != endx; x = (x + dx) & 0x7ff) {
851:				uint32_t color = fg;
852:				uint32_t oldcolor = fg;
853:
854:				if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_ROP && rop != OPENGL_LOGIC_OP_COPY)
855:					horrible_getputpixel(0, cpu, d, x, y, &oldcolor, dst_mode);
856:

858:				// (OpenBSD draws characters using this mechanism.)

866:					else
867:						draw = (pattern & 0x80000000UL)? 1 : 0;

896:							}

909:				pattern = (pattern << 1) | (pattern >> (nr_of_bits_in_the_middle-1));

913:				else
914:					src_x = (src_x + dx) & 0x7ff;
915:			}
925:		    (0x01) and DE_PRIM_RECTANGLE (0x03).

927:		    *** THE TWO KILLERS THAT MATTER ARE NOT EXOTIC VALUES: THEY
928:		    ARE NAMED PRIMITIVES, AND ONE OF THEM IS THE RESET STATE. ***
929:		    crmfbreg.h defines them as FULL 32-BIT VALUES, not top-byte
930:		    indices: DE_PRIM_POINT 0x00000000, LINE 0x01000000,
931:		    TRIANGLE 0x02000000, RECTANGLE 0x03000000.  Writing them
932:		    as "0x01" invites a `case 0x02:' that can NEVER match,
933:		    since the switch compares `op & 0xff000000'.  POINT and
934:		    TRIANGLE land
935:		    here.  And dev_sgi_re_init memsets the whole device struct,
936:		    so CRIME_DE_PRIMITIVE reads 0 -- which IS POINT -- until a
937:		    guest writes it.  A guest that sets the START bit having
938:		    never written the primitive register at all therefore died
939:		    here.  It need not choose a bad value; it need only omit a
940:		    good one.

942:		    Now a LATCHED DECLINE that falls out of the switch and
943:		    returns.  The diagnostic is kept in full -- it names the op
944:		    and the vertices, which is what a maintainer implementing
945:		    the primitive would want -- and gains `-- declined.  (once)'
946:		    so it cannot be mistaken for the old host kill and cannot
947:		    flood a guest loop.  */

954:		}

1269:/*
1270: *  SGI "mte", NetBSD sources describes it as a "memory transfer engine".
1271: *
1272: *  If the relative address has the 0x0800 (CRIME_DE_START) flag set, it means
1273: *  "go ahead with the transfer". Otherwise, it is just reads and writes of the
1274: *  registers.
1275: */

1277:void do_mte_transfer(struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data *d)
1278:{
1279:	uint32_t mode = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_MODE - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];

1285:	int32_t dst_y_step = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_DST_Y_STEP - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
1286:	uint32_t dstlen = dst1 - dst0 + 1, fill_addr;
1287:	unsigned char zerobuf[4096];

1289:	int src = (mode & MTE_MODE_SRC_BUF_MASK) >> MTE_SRC_TLB_SHIFT;
1290:	uint32_t bytemask = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_BYTEMASK - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
1291:	uint32_t bg = d->mte_reg[(CRIME_MTE_BG - 0x3000) / sizeof(uint32_t)];
1292:
1293:	debug("[ sgi_mte: STARTING: mode=0x%08x src0=0x%08x src1=0x%08x src_y_step=%i dst0=0x%08x,"
1294:	    " dst1=0x%08x dst_y_step=%i bg=0x%x bytemask=0x%x ]\n",
1295:	    mode,
1296:	    src0, src1, src_y_step,
1297:	    dst0, dst1, dst_y_step,
1298:	    bg, bytemask);
1299:
1300:	if (dst_y_step != 0 && dst_y_step != 1 && dst_y_step != -1) {
1301:		fatal("[ sgi_mte: TODO! unimplemented dst_y_step %i ]", dst_y_step);
1302:		// exit(1);
1303:	}

1442:			if (starting_page != ending_page) {

import re, glob, os
#  The sgirebufuninit signature: a stack buffer passed to memory_rw with a length that is
#  NOT sizeof(buf) and not a literal -- i.e. a length the GUEST may influence, where the
#  fill and the transfer can disagree.
call = re.compile(r'memory_rw\s*\((.*?)\)\s*;', re.S)
hits = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob('*.c')):
    src = open(f, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read()
    # stack byte buffers declared in this file
    bufs = set(re.findall(r'\b(?:unsigned char|uint8_t)\s+(\w+)\s*\[\s*\d+\s*\]\s*;', src))
    if not bufs:
        continue
    for m in call.finditer(src):
        args = [a.strip() for a in m.group(1).split(',')]
        if len(args) < 6:
            continue
        databuf, ln = args[3], args[4]
        nm = re.sub(r'^&', '', databuf).split('[')[0]
        if nm not in bufs:
            continue
        if ln.startswith('sizeof') or re.fullmatch(r'(0x)?[0-9]+', ln):
            continue          # transfer length pinned to the buffer -> not this class
        line = src[:m.start()].count('\n') + 1
        hits.append((f, line, nm, ln, args[5]))
print("%-26s %6s %-12s %-16s %s" % ("file", "line", "buffer", "length", "dir"))
for h in hits:
    print("%-26s %6d %-12s %-16s %s" % h)
print("\n%d call sites where the transfer length is NOT pinned to the buffer" % len(hits))

import re, glob
#  LIMIT 3 of the length scanner: a site passing sizeof(buf) is excluded by construction, yet a
#  sizeof(buf) transfer of a PARTIALLY FILLED buffer is the same defect.  This closes that
#  direction: every memory_rw that WRITES a stack byte-buffer into guest-visible memory, with
#  whether the enclosing function ever initialises that buffer wholesale.
call = re.compile(r'memory_rw\s*\((.*?)\)\s*;', re.S)
fn   = re.compile(r'^[A-Za-z_][\w \t\*]*\b(\w+)\s*\([^;]*\)\s*\{', re.M)
rows = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob('*.c')):
    src = open(f, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read()
    decls = dict(re.findall(r'\b(?:unsigned char|uint8_t)\s+(\w+)\s*\[\s*(\d+)\s*\]\s*;', src))
    if not decls: continue
    fns = [(m.start(), m.group(1)) for m in fn.finditer(src)]
    for m in call.finditer(src):
        args = [a.strip() for a in m.group(1).split(',')]
        if len(args) < 6: continue
        nm = re.sub(r'^&', '', args[3]).split('[')[0]
        if nm not in decls: continue
        if 'MEM_WRITE' not in args[5]: continue      # writes INTO guest-visible memory
        owner = 'file-scope'
        for pos, name in fns:
            if pos < m.start(): owner = name
        # does anything initialise the whole buffer?
        init = bool(re.search(r'memset\s*\(\s*&?' + nm + r'\b', src)) or \
               bool(re.search(r'\b(?:unsigned char|uint8_t)\s+' + nm + r'\s*\[\s*\d+\s*\]\s*=', src))
        line = src[:m.start()].count('\n') + 1
        rows.append((f, line, owner, nm, decls[nm], args[4], "yes" if init else "NO"))
print("%-24s %5s %-26s %-10s %5s %-14s %s" % ("file","line","function","buffer","size","len","wholesale init?"))
for r in rows: print("%-24s %5d %-26s %-10s %5s %-14s %s" % r)
print("\n%d MEM_WRITE sites from a stack byte-buffer; %d with NO wholesale initialisation"
      % (len(rows), sum(1 for r in rows if r[6] == "NO")))

1:#!/usr/bin/env python3
2:#
3:#  sgirandompixel -- RUNG-3 WITNESS for dev_sgi_re.c horrible_getputpixel().
4:#
5:#  THE DEFECT.  horrible_getputpixel() extracts `tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7' and switches on it.
6:#  Cases 0, 1, 2 and 4 are implemented.  The `default:' arm prints, has its exit(1) COMMENTED OUT,
7:#  and then executes `*color = random(); return;'.  So a failed GET does not decline -- it hands the
8:#  caller a random 32-bit value.  In draw_primitive() the failed source GET and the destination PUT
9:#  share one `color' variable in one loop iteration:
10:#
11:#      if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN)
12:#              horrible_getputpixel(0, ..., src_x, src_y, &color, src_mode);   // may set random()
13:#      ...
14:#      if (draw)
15:#              horrible_getputpixel(1, ..., x, y, &color, dst_mode);           // writes it
16:#
17:#  AND THE UNHANDLED VALUE IS A DOCUMENTED MODE.  src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h defines five:
18:#    DE_MODE_TLB_A 0x0000 -> 0 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_A 0x1000 -> 4 handled
19:#    DE_MODE_TLB_B 0x0400 -> 1 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_B 0x1400 -> 5 *** default -> random() ***
20:#    DE_MODE_TLB_C 0x0800 -> 2 handled
21:#
22:#  CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this witness catches and what it does NOT:
23:#    CATCHES   the default arm substituting a value instead of declining: `copy lin_b' reds if the
24:#              destination receives anything other than a value distinct from BOTH the source and
25:#              the pre-painted destination.  Restoring `exit(1)' there kills the machine and reds
26:#              every row.  Making the arm leave *color untouched turns `copy lin_b' green-by-accident
27:#              ONLY if the pre-paint is removed -- which is why the destination is PRE-PAINTED with a
28:#              known value and the row rejects it: "left alone" and "wrote random" are then distinct.
29:#    DOES NOT  establish that any real driver selects LIN_B.  It appears only in crmfbreg.h and the
30:#              function's own comment; the PROM's MTE zero-fill path does not call this function; and
31:#              there is no O2/sgimips image in the tree to boot.  **Documented and storable is NOT
32:#              driver-selected**, and this probe claims only the former (grok, the witness panel).
33:#
34:#  DESIGN CREDIT AND THE FALSE NEGATIVE IT AVOIDS (grok, panel_20260906_224008).  The obvious probe --
35:#  set the source mode to LIN_B, draw, read the destination -- FINDS NOTHING.  At reset the
36:#  destination mode is TLB_A and re_tlb_a[] has bit 31 clear, so the PUT returns WITHOUT WRITING and
37:#  the destination is unchanged: a false negative that looks like a clean run.  Using LIN_A as the
38:#  DESTINATION closes it, because case 4 forces the valid bit:
39:#      tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[x >> 12] << 12);  ofs = x & 4095;
40:#  so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10 puts the tile at physical 0x10000, and the byte offset is x itself.
41:#
42:#  THE ORACLE IS CERTAIN, NOT PROBABILISTIC.  glibc random() is 31-bit, so the value written has bit
43:#  31 CLEAR and can never equal a source of 0xAABBCCDD.  Inequality is guaranteed, not 2^-32.
44:#
45:#  usage: sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]

102:def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.6):
103:    pid, fd = pty.fork()
104:    if pid == 0:
105:        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64",
106:                       "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])
107:        os._exit(127)
108:    buf = ""
109:    def rd(t):
110:        nonlocal buf
111:        end = time.time() + t
112:        while time.time() < end:
113:            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
114:            if not r:
115:                continue
116:            try:
117:                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
118:                if not d:
119:                    return False
120:                buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
121:            except OSError:
122:                return False
123:        return True
124:    alive = rd(settle)
125:    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
126:        try:
127:            os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
128:        except OSError:
129:            alive = False
130:            break
131:        if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.22):
132:            alive = False
133:            break
134:    try:
135:        os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
136:    except OSError:
137:        pass
138:    try:
139:        os.close(fd)
140:    except OSError:
141:        pass
142:    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive

144:def printed(t, reg):
145:    #  MASK TO 32 BITS.  `lw' sign-extends into a 64-bit register, so a value with bit 31 set
146:    #  reads back as 0xffffffffaabbccdd and a row comparing raw strings fails on FORMATTING
147:    #  rather than on behaviour -- measured on this probe's first run.  The shipped
148:    #  sgi_re_width_probe.py carries the same normalisation for the same reason.
149:    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
150:    return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % (int(m.group(1), 16) & 0xffffffff)

152:class Addr(object):
153:    def __init__(self, base): self.a = base
154:    def take(self, n):
155:        got = self.a; self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20; return got

157:def run_ops(ops, at):
158:    put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
159:    put += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
160:            "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP)]
161:    return put + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(ops)]
162:
163:def store(A, addr, val, op=OP_SW_T0_A3):
164:    return ["a3=0x%x" % addr, "t0=0x%x" % val] + run_ops([op], A.take(1))
165:
166:SENTINEL = 0xdeadbeef
167:def load(A, addr):
168:    return ["at=0x%x" % addr, "v0=0x%x" % SENTINEL] + run_ops([OP_LW_V0_AT], A.take(1))

170:def setup(A, src_mode):
171:    """Program the engine, paint source and destination, then fire one rectangle.
172:       Every store is a REAL GUEST INSTRUCTION through memory_rw -- no device_add,
173:       no debugger `put' to a device register."""
174:    #  *** THE 64-BIT STORE WRITES THE HIGH WORD TO [n] AND THE LOW WORD TO [n+1]. ***  Measured:
175:    #  `sd' of 0x10 gave `re_linear_a[0] = 0, [1] = 0x10', so the tile pointer was 0 and every pixel
176:    #  went to PHYSICAL ADDRESS 0 -- the destination stayed at its pre-paint and the ACCEPT control
177:    #  failed, which is precisely the "do not leave it 0: that writes phys 0" trap grok named.  The
178:    #  value below puts 0x10 in the HIGH word so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10.
179:    c  = store(A, RE_LINEAR_A, 0x0000001000000000, op=OP_SD_T0_A3)  # sd: a 4-byte sw is DECLINED here
180:    c += store(A, DST_KSEG1, DST_PAINT)
181:    c += store(A, SRC_KSEG1, SRC_VAL)
182:    c += store(A, DE_MODE_DST,   MODE_LIN_A)
183:    c += store(A, DE_MODE_SRC,   src_mode)
184:    c += store(A, DE_DRAWMODE,   DRAWMODE)
185:    c += store(A, DE_XFER_ADDR,  SRC_OFS)              # linear: src_x = addr_src, src_y = 0
186:    c += store(A, DE_XFER_STEPX, 4)                    # must equal src bufdepth or the XFER arm warns
187:    c += store(A, DE_FG,         FG_VAL)               # what a declined GET leaves in `color'
188:    c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_0,  0x00000000)           # (x1,y1) = (0,0)
189:    #  *** (x2,y2) = (0,3): ONE COLUMN, FOUR ROWS.  The height is load-bearing and the width must
190:    #  NOT be used instead. ***  `lin_b diag once' pins that the announce is LATCHED, and its channel
191:    #  is the announcement COUNT -- which cannot vary at all if the rectangle is one pixel, because an
192:    #  unlatched announce then also fires exactly once.  Measured: with (0,0)-(0,0) the M_NOLATCH
193:    #  mutant scored 8/8, a clean escape, and the row was VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION.
194:    #  It is the HEIGHT and not the width because LIN_A addressing is `ofs = x & 4095' -- y is
195:    #  ignored -- so four ROWS write the same byte four times (the value oracle survives) while four
196:    #  COLUMNS would write overlapping bytes at ofs 0..3 and destroy it.
197:    c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_1,  0x00000003)           # (x2,y2) = (0,3): 4 pixels, all at ofs 0
198:    c += store(A, DE_PRIM_START, PRIM_RECT)            # PRIMITIVE|START fires the draw
199:    return c

205:A = Addr(CODE)
206:t, alive = session(store(A, SCRATCH, LIVE_VAL) + load(A, SCRATCH) + ["print v0"])
207:row("live", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL,
208:    "LIVENESS: guest store then load, through RAM")
209:
210:A = Addr(CODE)
211:t, alive = session(load(A, DE_STATUS) + ["print v0"])
212:row("devsig", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", DE_STAT,
213:    "DEVSIG: CRIME_DE_STATUS reads 0x1e000000; RAM reads 0")
214:
215:# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the accept control
216:#  Both modes LIN_A: the copy is the operation the defect corrupts.  If this row does not
217:#  land the source value, the probe has not driven the engine and the defect row below is
218:#  VACUOUS -- which is why it is a row and not an assumption.
219:A = Addr(CODE)
220:t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_LIN_A) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
221:row("copy lin_a", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", "0x%x" % SRC_VAL,
222:    "ACCEPT: LIN_A source copies to the destination")
223:
224:# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the defect
225:#  LIN_B source: the GET hits `default:', sets *color = random(), and the PUT writes it.
226:#  Two rows, because one is not enough: the value must differ from the SOURCE (so the copy
227:#  did not happen) AND from the PRE-PAINT (so the destination was actually written).
228:A = Addr(CODE)
229:t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_LIN_B) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
230:got_b = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
231:row("lin_b not src", got_b, "0x%x" % SRC_VAL,
232:    "the copy did not happen -- true before AND after the fix", negate=True)
233:#  *** THE INVERTING ROW.  It is RED on the committed build (which writes random()) and GREEN once the
234:#  `*color = random()' assignment is deleted, because the caller's `uint32_t color = fg' then stands.
235:#  It also discriminates every REJECTED disposition: zeroing leaves 0, suppressing the PUT leaves the
236:#  pre-paint, implementing LIN_B leaves the source.  Only "leave *color alone" leaves FG.
237:row("lin_b is fg", got_b, "0x%x" % FG_VAL,
238:    "FIX: a declined GET leaves *color, so the PUT writes FG")
239:row("lin_b alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive",
240:    "the host survives: the arm's exit(1) is commented out")
241:
242:#  ATTRIBUTION: the value could only have come from this device's default arm.
243:#  ATTRIBUTION and the LATCH, in one row.  Bare presence was enough for a witness; for the fix it must
244:#  also pin that the announce is BOUNDED -- the house idiom is N = 1 per device instance with `(once)' in
245:#  the message text, and this helper is PER-PIXEL, so an unlatched announce is a per-pixel flood.  This
246:#  row is the discriminator against a SILENT decline (member 2), which would emit nothing at all.
247:n_diag = t.count("unimplemented dst_mode")
248:row("lin_b diag", "yes" if n_diag else "no", "yes",
249:    "ATTRIBUTION: the arm's own diagnostic; RAM emits no text")
250:row("lin_b diag once", "%d" % n_diag, "1",
251:    "LATCH: one announcement for FOUR pixels, not one each")
252:
253:ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
254:print("")
255:print("SGIRAND_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
256:print("SGIRAND_WITNESS=%s" % ("PASS" if ok == len(rows) else "FAIL"))
257:print("SGIRAND_DONE")
258:sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)

156: *  NOTE: This is very slow, even slower than the normal emulated framebuffer,

163: *  An exception is how Linux/O2 uses the framebuffer, in a "tweaked" mode

1271: *
1272: *  If the relative address has the 0x0800 (CRIME_DE_START) flag set, it means

Two more findings affect the review: the MTE caller measures depth in bits but compares it with `4`, leaving its RGBA branch unreachable; and the fill scanner accepts any `memset` with the same buffer name anywhere in the file. Neither supports the brief’s claim that the class scan is closed.

111:
112:void get_rgb(struct sgi_gbe_data *d, uint32_t color, uint8_t* r, uint8_t* g, uint8_t* b)
113:{
114:	// TODO: Don't switch on color_mode. For overlays, this is always 
115:	// 8-bit index mode!
116:	switch (d->color_mode) {
117:	case CRMFB_MODE_TYP_I8:
118:		color &= 0xff;
119:		*r = d->selected_palette[color] >> 24;
120:		*g = d->selected_palette[color] >> 16;
121:		*b = d->selected_palette[color] >>  8;
122:		break;
123:	case CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RG3B2:	// Used by NetBSD console mode
124:		*r = 255 * ((color >> 5) & 7) / 7;
125:		*g = 255 * ((color >> 2) & 7) / 7;
126:		*b = (color & 3) * 85;
127:		break;
128:	case CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8:	// Used by NetBSD's X11 server
129:		*r = color >> 24;
130:		*g = color >> 16;
131:		*b = color >>  8;
132:		break;
133:	default:/*  #196: (Codex/Fable) d->color_mode is guest-controlled; an
134:		    unimplemented WID mode must not exit() the host (get_rgb is
135:		    per-pixel, so don't fatal()-flood either) -- render black.  */
136:		*r = *g = *b = 0;
137:		break;
138:	}
139:}

453:
