GO-WITH-CHANGES

Full-buffer initialization is justified. The proposed witness, exhaustive-scope claim, and severity argument need correction before this round can claim closure.

I checked `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`; the frozen enclosing function matches the working tree. However, the preceding `sgirandompixel` change and its probe are staged here, so describing that apparatus as already committed is inaccurate.

**Q1. The central finding survives, with several premise corrections.**

- **Initialization:** There is no full initialization of `buf`. “Uninitialized on every path” overstates this: matched operations initialize every byte they consume. The checked device Makefile has no automatic-variable initialization flag; I have not established the flags of a particular executable.
- **#95:** Its device pre-zero explicitly requires `MEM_READ` and clears exactly `len`. It covers neither an outgoing write buffer nor bytes beyond the requested read length. Its “whole class” claim needs narrowing. [Memory dispatch](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485)
- **RAM versus device:** The existing probe’s source and destination resolve to ordinary low physical RAM on IP32. More generally, the helper’s translated address can identify RAM, a device, or unmapped space. **RAM does not inherently make Path B worse:** a successful RAM read defines the requested prefix through `memcpy`; the missing suffix is the same problem. [RAM access](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:778)
- **Field independence:** The DE register handler stores the mode words without reconciling these fields. The XFER step check merely announces a mismatch and continues. But **not all seven call sites receive those register words**: the two MTE sites construct their own modes. [DE register handling](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:978)
- **Depth:** All four field values survive the DE producer. However, the header names only buffer depths 8, 16, and 32; it does **not** document the fourth encoding as a supported 64-bit mode. Also, Path A does not depend entirely on that fourth encoding: the CI cases already contradict that assertion.
- **YCRCB:** It is named in the header and has no implementation arm here. A named format does not establish that every pairing with another field is hardware-valid. The same header also defines **pixel-depth and double-buffer fields**, which the brief’s compatibility discussion omits. [Mode definitions](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h:319)

I enumerated the four transfer widths against each switch arm. These are the byte indices consumed without initialization by that operation, assuming the memory read supplies its requested prefix:

| Transfer bytes | PUT, CI | PUT, other arms | GET, CI | GET, other arms |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | None | None | None | 1, 2, 3 |
| 2 | 1 | None | None | 2, 3 |
| 4 | 1, 2, 3 | None | None | None |
| 8 | 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 | 4, 5, 6, 7 | None | None |

RGB GET accesses indices 1, 2, 3, rather than all four bytes; that distinction does not change this table. “None” says nothing about the separate random-color behavior. [Buffer and conversions](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:284)

**Q2. The existing `copy lin_a` row is insensitive to the initialization-size mistakes you named.**

It exercises matched four-byte operation, so it cannot establish initialization outside that footprint. A PUT-only detector also cannot establish initialization of bytes consumed beyond a short GET.

Require separate assertions for initialization and exact resulting values in both directions. A check that merely finds a `memset`, or observes repeatable output, establishes neither. These are predicted coverage gaps; no new detector or measured mutation results for this defect were present in the reviewed artifacts.

**Q3. Choose full-object initialization, and correct the option descriptions.**

`uint8_t buf[8] = {0};` is another spelling of option 1. It is the smallest defensible repair because it defines missing bytes while retaining the existing addressing and transfer widths. Describe zero padding as an emulator safety policy; hardware fidelity remains unestablished. The per-pixel performance cost is unmeasured.

The closest concrete precedent I found is MEC’s full-array clear before descriptor assignments. GBE also deliberately produces black for an unsupported color mode. Neither establishes the hardware compatibility matrix for these fields. [MEC initialization](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_mec.c:194), [GBE fallback](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:133)

Your five observable outcomes are not correctly characterized:

- **Option 2 remains incomplete.** It does not give Path B a defined zero result.
- **Option 3 specifies only PUT.** Padding need not mean replication, and it supplies no GET policy.
- **Option 4 does not automatically preserve the destination.** Returning from GET leaves the caller free to perform its dependent PUT. Preserving the destination requires failure propagation through the callers.
- **Option 5 can change addressing.** `bufdepth` participates in tile width and offset calculation. A downward clamp also cannot supply missing GET bytes.

A missing semantic option is proper width-aware packing and unpacking, including the other pixel-format fields. That needs hardware evidence and is substantially broader than initializing this buffer.

**Q4. Reject the proposed witness as a dependable oracle.**

The three listed mode decodes agree with the source. They do not establish a working detector.

The existing probe uses its LIN_A constant for the destination and matched control as well as a source case. Changing that shared constant changes the observation path and the control simultaneously. Its assertions and diagnostics also target the previous defect. This is more than a one-constant detector change. [Probe setup and assertions](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py:170)

The known-previous-pixel proposal is unsound as a portable oracle. Separate automatic objects have no architectural promise of retaining each other’s contents. Register allocation, inlining, and intervening calls matter. **Path A has the same problem:** observing an unassigned byte does not guarantee that its value differs from the intended padding.

Use initializedness analysis alongside exact functional assertions. MemorySanitizer tracks uninitialized use, but its instrumentation coverage must be established; merely copying undefined bytes need not produce an immediate diagnostic. [MemorySanitizer documentation](https://clang.llvm.org/docs/MemorySanitizer.html), [Memcheck’s definedness model](https://valgrind.org/docs/manual/mc-manual.html#mc-manual.uninitvals)

The missing measurement is an attributed initializedness result for each direction, tied to the reviewed build, together with passing functional controls. No rung-3 attempt was completed in this static review, so that mandatory prerequisite remains outstanding.

Also remove “LIN_A cannot be refused”: its index guard can return before the buffer code.

**Q5. I would record MEDIUM provisionally, with potential host information disclosure explicit.**

HIGH is not established by inheriting #95’s severity. The missing evidence concerns sensitive-data exposure and practical impact.

Equally, “usually the previous pixel” and “first call in a frame” are unsupported explanations of stack contents. They cannot justify downgrading this to a cosmetic rendering defect.

**Q6. Keep `sgirandomcolour` separate.**

“Deterministic across runs” is too weak a shared oracle. A stable unintended value, unchanged destination, or repeatable pseudorandom sequence could satisfy it. Full initialization also leaves the deliberate `random()` assignments intact.

One buffer-initialization repair can cover A and B. The random-color behavior needs its own explicit disposition and assertions; proximity in the switch does not meet the stated joining rule.

**The scope census is not closed.**

The second scanner’s initialization check searches the entire file for the buffer name. It checks neither size, execution order, nor control-flow coverage. Its function label does not constrain that search.

I found four omitted, safe stack-buffer write sites:

- DEC21143 descriptor writebacks at lines 474 and 842, initialized through `store_le32`. Nested argument commas defeat the scanner’s splitting.
- Maple response writes at lines 291 and 369, whose casts prevent recognition of the buffer argument.

[DEC21143 writeback](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_dec21143.c:466), [Maple response](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_dreamcast_maple.c:285)

Thus “five write-a-stack-buffer sites” is already an incomplete denominator. Agreement between scanners sharing parsing limitations is not exhaustive corroboration.

Two “SAFE” claims also need narrowing:

- **GBE:** Its producer keeps the divisor nonzero and bounds the transfer, but conversion still loops to 512 after a shorter read. That can consume unfilled bytes. The smaller forwarded framebuffer prefix prevents me from calling this a demonstrated guest disclosure; it nevertheless invalidates the blanket initialization-safety claim.
- **PXG:** #207 bounds the later SRAM copy’s source span without correspondingly bounding consumption. The initial SRAM copy remains unchecked. This does not invalidate the enumerated PX RAM transfer maximum of 3080 bytes; it invalidates treating that maximum as proof of complete initialization across both branches.

**Two additional correctness findings deserve separate records.**

The MTE producer enumerates depths **8, 16, 32, 64**, then tests `depth == 4`. That condition is never true, so its intended RGBA construction is dead and its helper calls retain CI. This directly refutes the assertion that all callers supply the same independently programmed mode pair. [MTE producer](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:1288)

RGB serialization and deserialization also disagree: storing `0x11223300` produces bytes `11 22 33 00`, which the RGB GET arm recombines as `0x22330000`. Calling all implemented color arms “correct” is unwarranted.

No files were changed, and no gates, probes, or mutants were run.

Full-buffer initialization is justified. The proposed witness, exhaustive-scope claim, and severity argument need correction before this round can claim closure.

I checked `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`; the frozen enclosing function matches the working tree. However, the preceding `sgirandompixel` change and its probe are staged here, so describing that apparatus as already committed is inaccurate.

**Q1. The central finding survives, with several premise corrections.**

- **Initialization:** There is no full initialization of `buf`. “Uninitialized on every path” overstates this: matched operations initialize every byte they consume. The checked device Makefile has no automatic-variable initialization flag; I have not established the flags of a particular executable.
- **#95:** Its device pre-zero explicitly requires `MEM_READ` and clears exactly `len`. It covers neither an outgoing write buffer nor bytes beyond the requested read length. Its “whole class” claim needs narrowing. [Memory dispatch](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485)
- **RAM versus device:** The existing probe’s source and destination resolve to ordinary low physical RAM on IP32. More generally, the helper’s translated address can identify RAM, a device, or unmapped space. **RAM does not inherently make Path B worse:** a successful RAM read defines the requested prefix through `memcpy`; the missing suffix is the same problem. [RAM access](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:778)
- **Field independence:** The DE register handler stores the mode words without reconciling these fields. The XFER step check merely announces a mismatch and continues. But **not all seven call sites receive those register words**: the two MTE sites construct their own modes. [DE register handling](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:978)
- **Depth:** All four field values survive the DE producer. However, the header names only buffer depths 8, 16, and 32; it does **not** document the fourth encoding as a supported 64-bit mode. Also, Path A does not depend entirely on that fourth encoding: the CI cases already contradict that assertion.
- **YCRCB:** It is named in the header and has no implementation arm here. A named format does not establish that every pairing with another field is hardware-valid. The same header also defines **pixel-depth and double-buffer fields**, which the brief’s compatibility discussion omits. [Mode definitions](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h:319)

I enumerated the four transfer widths against each switch arm. These are the byte indices consumed without initialization by that operation, assuming the memory read supplies its requested prefix:

| Transfer bytes | PUT, CI | PUT, other arms | GET, CI | GET, other arms |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | None | None | None | 1, 2, 3 |
| 2 | 1 | None | None | 2, 3 |
| 4 | 1, 2, 3 | None | None | None |
| 8 | 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 | 4, 5, 6, 7 | None | None |

RGB GET accesses indices 1, 2, 3, rather than all four bytes; that distinction does not change this table. “None” says nothing about the separate random-color behavior. [Buffer and conversions](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:284)

It exercises matched four-byte operation, so it cannot establish initialization outside that footprint. A PUT-only detector also cannot establish initialization of bytes consumed beyond a short GET.

Require separate assertions for initialization and exact resulting values in both directions. A check that merely finds a `memset`, or observes repeatable output, establishes neither. These are predicted coverage gaps; no new detector or measured mutation results for this defect were present in the reviewed artifacts.

**Q3. Choose full-object initialization, and correct the option descriptions.**

Describe zero padding as an emulator safety policy; hardware fidelity remains unestablished. The per-pixel performance cost is unmeasured.

The closest concrete precedent I found is MEC’s full-array clear before descriptor assignments. GBE also deliberately produces black for an unsupported color mode. Neither establishes the hardware compatibility matrix for these fields. [MEC initialization](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_mec.c:194), [GBE fallback](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:133)
